# Ask HN: Any interesting books you have read lately? — source 8

Analysis dated **2026-10-17, 23:16:41 +02:00**, Europe/Warsaw. [Original thread](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=37156372).

**228 nonfiction assessment units: 208 scored, non-vetoed entries; 19 vetoes with full counterfactual scores; one unresolved study guide.** Every non-vetoed source entry is retained below and in the main index, including poor fits. Three entries are series-level units, not invented individual volumes. Fiction and non-book references are excluded before scoring.

The strongest general reading choices are **Material World**, **The New Lombard Street**, **Thinking in Systems**, **The Machine That Changed the World**, **Chip War**, and **The Prize**. For direct technical use, start with **Designing Data-Intensive Applications**, **Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach**, or **Rust Atomics and Locks**. High scores reflect the requested mechanisms and utility, not literary prestige or broad public appeal. Whole-book scoring confidence is moderate at best; one-point differences should not be read as measured precision.

## Source coverage and method

The [official HN item](https://hacker-news.firebaseio.com/v0/item/37156372.json) reports **520 comments**. A recursive traversal visited **532 nodes**, including the root: **520 readable comments**, **7 deleted records**, and **4 dead/flagged marker records**. The [Algolia thread tree](https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/items/37156372) contains the same 520 readable comment IDs. Every returned child ID was traversed; nested replies, critical comments, the opening post and the in-thread compilation were considered. No browser expansion was needed because the two APIs expose the reply tree directly. Deleted or flagged text was not recoverable. **Capture coverage confidence: high for the readable snapshot; not a claim about recoverable deleted text or later edits.**

Titles come only from that source. External pages resolve identity, publication, genre, reception, editions and contested claims; their additional recommended titles are not added. Mere author mentions do not create books. Each assessment links its originating comment. Repeated mentions and the commenter’s compilation are not independent quality evidence. The second audit recovered four short recommendations missed in the first pass: A Path Through the Jungle, Sacred Economics, Artificial Intelligence: A Modern Approach, and In Defense of Civilization.

The assessment separates three questions: (1) Is this a nonfiction book or an identified series? (2) Is a serious veto established? (3) How much reliable, reusable value does it offer this reader? A low score, an unwanted subject, political advocacy, religious vocabulary, or self-help packaging is not an additional exclusion. Criticism must concern the book’s actual claims. I distinguish controlled evidence, specialist criticism, author replies and unverified reader reports. These are researched screening judgments, not claims that every book was read in full. **Score confidence: M = moderate, L = low, U = unknown.** Veto confidence is stated separately.

## Scoring contract

| Code | Criterion | Maximum |
|---|---|---|
| E | Evidence and inferential integrity | 20 |
| M | Mechanistic and causal depth | 17 |
| U | Transferable strategic utility | 15 |
| S | Systems integration | 12 |
| D | Information density and length justification | 10 |
| R | Durability and resistance to hype | 9 |
| F | Direct domain fit | 7 |
| A | Expert authority and intellectual standing | 5 |
| N | Narrative architecture and clarity | 3 |
| P | Authorial precision and finesse | 2 |
| T | Predominant specified negative theme | 0 or −10 |

**Total = E + M + U + S + D + R + F + A + N + P + T.** Each criterion is allocated directly on its weighted scale. T is applied once, even when several listed themes overlap. Unwanted topics not in criterion 11, such as sport or generic self-help, reduce fit but do not invent another deduction. A minority-identity subject alone is not automatically racial history or a civil movement. A technical or scientific biography is exempt from the no-payload veto when applicable, not automatically exempt from T.

Verdict bands: **85–100 Recommend; 75–84 Consider; 65–74 Selective; 50–64 Low priority; below 50 Poor fit.** Veto overrides every band: **V1** epistemic failure; **V2** ideological capture; **V3** no transferable intellectual payload; **V4** hype or ephemerality dependence. Counterfactual scores suspend only this gate. They preserve the identified flaws and never make a vetoed book recommended.

## Ranked source list — five columns

**GR = Goodreads /5, followed by the number of ratings.** Each figure links the retrieved record; these are cached snapshots retrieved on 2026-10-17, not synchronized live counts. Different editions can share a work rating. No invented average combines Goodreads, Amazon, StoryGraph, LibraryThing or Book Marks; an identified Goodreads record supplies the compact reader score here. NR means no suitable verified rating, an unspecified series/canon, or unresolved identity. Counts as small as 2 or 12 are shown and must not be treated as reliable consensus. Ratings do not enter the quality score.

Title links use Amazon.de. Unmarked new edition links identify a catalogued paperback; **HC** marks the confirmed hardcover used when no suitable paperback was verified. **†** is an explicit title/ISBN search, not a verified product listing. 25 entries use searches, including series and unresolved editions. Earlier edition links are retained where applicable. ISBN identity and binding checks do not establish current Amazon stock, delivery, language selection or seller condition. Later paperback editions can differ from the first publication year shown. Identity and edition qualifications are in the linked assessment.

The most severe failures are the pseudo-historical Anunnaki claims, the numerical truth-testing method in Power vs. Force, and the racial ideology in Mein Kampf. The Farfarers fails a more specific archaeological inference check. Goodbye to a River, Up in the Old Hotel, Travels in the Americas, and Lexicon Urthus fail the transferable-payload gate for this task; that does not deny their literary or reference value.

Never Split the Difference loses E1 and D2 for the reported selective success narrative and padding; No More Mr. Nice Guy loses E2 and D1 for specific, unverified causal assertions and framing; The Dawn of Everything loses E1, D1 and P1 after considering the detailed criticism and defenses together. These are low-confidence revaluations, not proof of fraud. The Bible’s former V1 gate assumed that the unspecified collection had been offered as one empirical theory. That assumption is not supported here: removing it increases recognition of its diverse historical, argumentative and institutional payload while keeping its evidence and domain-fit scores low.

## Fiction and non-book audit

The fiction gate applies even when a novel teaches useful engineering, business or philosophical ideas. Thus **The Goal**, **The Goal: A Business Graphic Novel**, **It’s Not Luck**, **Critical Chain**, **The Phoenix Project**, **Goldratt’s Rules of Flow**, and **Built to Sell** receive no quality score and are absent from both book indexes. Goldratt’s Rules of Flow is confirmed as a fictional business narrative in the [publisher’s description](https://www.routledge.com/Goldratts-Rules-of-Flow/Goldratt-Ashlag/p/book/9781032580067). **When We Cease to Understand the World** and Maalouf’s **Leo Africanus** are historical fiction, unlike the nonfiction source works about science or African geography. Zen and the Art of Motorcycle Maintenance is retained with its separate genre qualification.

Other named fiction, fictional series, and short-fiction collections are consolidated below. Variant spellings and shortened names in comments are normalized; unnamed installments are not expanded into fabricated title lists. The inventory is an exclusion audit, not a scored recommendation list.

- *The Utopians trilogy*; *The Water Knife*; *The Windup Girl*; *Accelerando*; *Postsingular*; *The Laundry Files*.
- *Equoid*; *Ukridge*; *Blandings*; *Jeeves*; *Psmith*; *Leave It to Psmith*.
- *Love Among the Chickens*; *Mike and Psmith*; *Psmith in the City*; *Psmith, Journalist*; *Three Men in a Boat*; *The Thursday Murder Club*.
- *Death and Croissants*; *A Confederacy of Dunces*; *The Left Hand of Darkness*; *The Word for World Is Forest*; *A Fire Upon the Deep*; *Earthsea*.
- *Vorkosigan Saga*; *Terraform: Watch/Worlds/Burn*; *The Three-Body Problem*; *The Dark Forest*; *Lord of Mysteries*; *Exhalation*.
- *The Goal*; *The Goal: A Business Graphic Novel*; *The Phoenix Project*; *Goldratt's Rules of Flow*; *It's Not Luck*; *Critical Chain*.
- *Fatherland*; *The Ghost*; *The Fear Index*; *The Second Sleep*; *Act of Oblivion*; *Cicero trilogy*.

**Non-books and non-identifiable references:** Butler Lampson’s *Hints and Principles for Computer System Design* is a paper, not a source book ([comment](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=37160732), [paper](https://arxiv.org/abs/2011.02455)); Goldratt’s *Standing on the Shoulders of Giants* is an article, even when appended to a book ([comment](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=37170073)); Jennifer Paxton’s *The Celtic World* is a Great Courses lecture course ([comment](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=37161294)). Podcasts, videos, blog posts, r/hfy, author-only recommendations, and the Tolstoy “War—What Is It Good For?” joke create no book entries. No title is inferred from an external author bibliography.

## Verification and remaining uncertainty

The generated indexes and this report were checked against one structured assessment record. All 227 numeric rows have 11 components, valid criterion limits and an exact total. All 209 non-vetoed entries occur once in the ranked list and main index, including the NR guide; all 19 current source vetoes occur in the veto index with full counterfactual vectors. All earlier source numbers are retained, and old analysis file hashes are unchanged. All originating comment IDs exist in the captured tree. Purchase ISBN check digits and source-record presence were checked; this does not test live stock.

The unresolved EPA guide cannot honestly receive a numerical score. Conditional identities include Global Business, Marx, Quantum Electrodynamics, the Aristotle ethics reference, The Long Game and The Complete Investor; genre qualifications apply to Pirsig and the French baseball history. Missing or edition-ambiguous reader ratings remain NR. [Structured assessment and capture manifest](data/hn37156372_20261017.json) records the source node IDs, totals, editions and decisions without reproducing the full discussion.
